And I suppose I do the same thing in the group planning meetings. I don't really mean to be rude, it's just... if I don't say it right then, I'm afraid I'll completely lose the thought. So I just say it.\n\nDoctor: Were you always this active and restless, even when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes. It's been a long time, but... well, even back in grade school, my teachers used to tell my mother I was bright, just... scattered. I guess I was always losing my school papers. And talking out of turn. They said I was always wiggling in my seat before I even finished my work. So... yes. I guess I was.\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty following instructions or paying attention to details when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. Even now, really. I help out sometimes with the papers at the community center, and... well, I'll miss a name on the sign-up sheet, or... write down the wrong room time on a note. Even when I'm sure I checked it. It just... happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong mood swings?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I mean, my mood seems pretty normal to me. I don't really have big swings or anything like that. Just... normal, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have physical complaints like headaches or stomachaches?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, just the usual aches you get at my age, I suppose. But no... I don't really get headaches or stomachaches. I feel fine in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're doing things you enjoy, like gardening or knitting?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose so. Quiet things... they don't really stay quiet for me. Even if I'm just knitting, I don't really sit calmly. I'll end up humming to myself, or... chatting. Or I keep getting up to look for my supplies. I just... have a hard time just sitting there, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don't really know why, it just... happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean... I don't really know what you mean by that. I just do what needs doing, mostly. I don't think I do things without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe a little. When I'm sitting in a meeting, or just watching television... I guess I'm usually tapping my foot. Or rubbing my fingers together, or just... fussing with whatever happens to be in my lap. I don't really think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Well... my husband says I do. He tells me I cut him off before he finishes talking. And I suppose I do the same thing in the group planning meetings. I don't really mean to be rude, it's just... if I don't say it right then, I'm afraid I'll completely lose the thought. So I just say it.\n\nDoctor: Were you always this active and restless, even when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes. It's been a long time, but... well, even back in grade school, my teachers used to tell my mother I was bright, just... scattered. I guess I was always losing my school papers. And talking out of turn. They said I was always wiggling in my seat before I even finished my work. So... yes. I guess I was.\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty following instructions or paying attention to details when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. Even now, really. I help out sometimes with the papers at the community center, and... well, I'll miss a name on the sign-up sheet, or... write down the wrong room time on a note. Even when I'm sure I checked it. It just... happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong mood swings?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I mean, my mood seems pretty normal to me. I don't really have big swings or anything like that. Just... normal, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have physical complaints like headaches or stomachaches?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, just the usual aches you get at my age, I suppose. But no... I don't really get headaches or stomachaches. I feel fine in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're doing things you enjoy, like gardening or knitting?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose so. Quiet things... they don't really stay quiet for me. Even if I'm just knitting, I don't really sit calmly. I'll end up humming to myself, or... chatting. Or I keep getting up to look for my supplies. I just... have a hard time just sitting there, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Did you have more problems with behavior, like acting out or being defiant, when you were a child?\n\nPatient: No... I don't believe so. That was a very long time ago, but... I don't remember getting into trouble like that. I was just... a normal girl, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks at home, similar to what you experience in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I manage the house just fine, I believe. I just... do what needs doing around the place. I haven't really noticed any trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these restless and fidgety feelings at work or when you're out in public, not just at home?\n\nPatient: Well... I don't really work anymore, so... I couldn't say about that. And when I'm out... I suppose I'm just going about my business. I haven't really paid much mind to it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just do what needs doing, mostly. I don't think I do things without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe a little. When I'm sitting in a meeting, or just watching television... I guess I'm usually tapping my foot. Or rubbing my fingers together, or just... fussing with whatever happens to be in my lap. I don't really think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Well... my husband says I do. He tells me I cut him off before he finishes talking. And I suppose I do the same thing in the group planning meetings. I don't really mean to be rude, it's just... if I don't say it right then, I'm afraid I'll completely lose the thought. So I just say it.\n\nDoctor: Were you always this active and restless, even when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes. It's been a long time, but... well, even back in grade school, my teachers used to tell my mother I was bright, just... scattered. I guess I was always losing my school papers. And talking out of turn. They said I was always wiggling in my seat before I even finished my work. So... yes. I guess I was.\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty following instructions or paying attention to details when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. Even now, really. I help out sometimes with the papers at the community center, and... well, I'll miss a name on the sign-up sheet, or... write down the wrong room time on a note. Even when I'm sure I checked it. It just... happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong mood swings?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I mean, my mood seems pretty normal to me. I don't really have big swings or anything like that. Just... normal, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have physical complaints like headaches or stomachaches?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, just the usual aches you get at my age, I suppose. But no... I don't really get headaches or stomachaches. I feel fine in that regard.\n\nDoctor: Do you also feel restless or fidgety when you're doing things you enjoy, like gardening or knitting?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose so. Quiet things... they don't really stay quiet for me. Even if I'm just knitting, I don't really sit calmly. I'll end up humming to myself, or... chatting. Or I keep getting up to look for my supplies. I just... have a hard time just sitting there, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Did you have more problems with behavior, like acting out or being defiant, when you were a child?\n\nPatient: No... I don't believe so. That was a very long time ago, but... I don't remember getting into trouble like that. I was just... a normal girl, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks at home, similar to what you experience in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I manage the house just fine, I believe. I just... do what needs doing around the place. I haven't really noticed any trouble with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these restless and fidgety feelings at work or when you're out in public, not just at home?\n\nPatient: Well... I don't really work anymore, so... I couldn't say about that. And when I'm out... I suppose I'm just going about my business. I haven't really paid much mind to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms of restlessness and difficulty focusing consistently throughout your adult life, not just in recent years?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it isn't just recent. It's... always been that way, really. Even back in grade school, I remember... my teachers telling my mother that I was bright, but... scattered. I was always losing my papers, or... talking out of turn. Wiggling around in my seat before I even finished my work... things like that. So... yes, I suppose it's been a long time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose my daughter thought I should come. She says I don't settle down much. I just... I feel like I'm always up and moving, I guess. Even when it's supposed to be a quiet day, I'm sort of... bouncing around. From the garden club list, to my knitting bag, to looking at travel papers... usually before I've really finished any one of them. It's just how I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble focusing on one task for a long time?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose so. If I'm at a planning meeting, or just trying to read the church bulletin... I can start out listening fine. But after a little while, I usually realize I've missed half of what was said. Or printed. I don't really know why, it just... happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean... I don't really know what you mean by that. I just do what needs doing, mostly. I don't think I do things without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe a little. When I'm sitting in a meeting, or just watching television... I guess I'm usually tapping my foot. Or rubbing my fingers together, or just... fussing with whatever happens to be in my lap. I don't really think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Well... my husband says I do. He tells me I cut him off before he finishes talking. And I suppose I do the same thing in the group planning meetings. I don't really mean to be rude, it's just... if I don't say it right then, I'm afraid I'll completely lose the thought. So I just say it.\n\nDoctor: Were you always this active and restless, even when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes. It's been a long time, but... well, even back in grade school, my teachers used to tell my mother I was bright, just... scattered. I guess I was always losing my school papers. I don't really have big swings or anything like that. Just... normal, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have physical complaints like headaches or stomachaches?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, just the usual aches you get at my age, I suppose. But no... I don't really get headaches or stomachaches. Quiet things... they don't really stay quiet for me. Even if I'm just knitting, I don't really sit calmly. I'll end up humming to myself, or... chatting. Or I keep getting up to look for my supplies. I just... have a hard time just sitting there, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Did you have more problems with behavior, like acting out or being defiant, when you were a child?\n\nPatient: No... I don't believe so. That was a very long time ago, but... I don't remember getting into trouble like that. I just do what needs doing, mostly. I don't think I do things without thinking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe a little. When I'm sitting in a meeting, or just watching television... I guess I'm usually tapping my foot. Or rubbing my fingers together, or just... fussing with whatever happens to be in my lap. And I suppose I do the same thing in the group planning meetings. I don't really mean to be rude, it's just... if I don't say it right then, I'm afraid I'll completely lose the thought.
